`cold-publication/1` emits chamber `STATE_AT_INSTANT degC` every 5 minutes including end; grid/generator/compressor/bus states on transitions and 5-minute heartbeat; compressor `INTERVAL_MEAN kW` per aligned 5-minute slot; door/start/transfer/power events on transition. `simulator/assetops_simulator/observation/transform.py` owns `cold-observation/1` hold/drop/fault transform variants, zero bias and default 3-second latency before Draft. The model retains one calibrated primary chamber probe per room; delete the two-primary-probe disagreement rule. Contradictory distinct GOOD controller outcomes for one attempt cause `CONTRADICTORY`. `cold-door-heat` property unit is `kWh/min`, with 0.02 per open minute; the equation's `*60` converts it to kW before its one-minute integration. The Lab persisted-state validator uses the `cold-thermal/1` finite vocabularies above, not the mini-grid generator lifecycle vocabulary; extend the closed identity/profile registries. `Role.value_kind` adds `EVENT`, and EVENT bindings use `conversion='IDENTITY'`, with typed outcome allowlist and no numeric conversion. Foundation controls stay NUMBER, not a new `Role.value_kind`.

The closed cold mapping uses `(device_id,signal_id)`: `(chamber-probe,temperature)` degC point, `(ambient-probe,temperature)` degC point, `(grid-meter,state)` state point, `(generator-controller,state)` state point, `(compressor-controller,state)` state point, `(backup-bus-meter,state)` state point, `(compressor-meter,power)` kW interval mean, `(door-sensor,event)` door events, `(generator-controller,start-event)` request/result events, `(transfer-controller,event)` transfer result, `(grid-meter,event)` loss/restored events, `(operator-log,temperature-event)` manual log. Each tuple is a separate versioned mapping entry with only its listed kinds, values and cadence. W2-00 also reserves EV entries `(charger-controller,session-event)` for session start/end/abort and `(connector-controller,fault-event)` for fault/clear, with connector/session identity rules above; W2-05b supplies the EV pack and its own frozen mapping version. The same signal ID on a different device is not an implicit alias (`simulator/assetops_simulator/gateway/mappings.py:22-46,87-107`).

## 5. W2-03 assessment, episodes and Findings

Analytic IDs `cold-excursion/1`, `cold-start-response/1`. Input selection is accepted evidence only, fixed half-open `[from,to)`, explicit `known_at` cutoff on server `received_at`/decision time, effective Foundation/binding versions, and source gate. Include exact accepted IDs and SHA, configuration IDs, model version, window/cutoff in sorted basis SHA. A reading is usable only if GOOD, calibrated, mapped to the room, and clock-attested; SUSPECT is context. Expected five-minute points: coverage = usable distinct scheduled timestamps / scheduled timestamps, capped at 1; `>=0.8` needed for an interval claim, but no gap >10 minutes may be bridged. Latest point must be <=10 minutes old at read time for a **current** state; historical window conclusion can remain bounded with explicit as-of. A failed worker or newer accepted relevant evidence marks prior projection `PENDING_RECOMPUTE` until a new immutable revision is written.

Excursion begins after two consecutive GOOD observations above upper band (or below lower band) at least 5 minutes apart; return requires two consecutive observations inside band by at least 0.2Â°C. The start is bounded by `[last in-band, first out-of-band]`; return by `[last out-of-band, first stable in-band]`; duration low = max(0, earliest return - latest start), high = latest return - earliest start, computed only across continuously covered spans. Initial out-of-band sample with no prior in-band gives unknown start; window end without return gives open episode and no finite high. `degree_minutes` bounds use observed rectangles only between adjacent usable samples <=10 minutes apart: low uses min adjacent exceedance, high max adjacent exceedance times interval minutes; add no unobserved gap area. Peak is the maximum **observed** value. An exposure Finding requires 15-minute lower duration and coverage >=0.8; otherwise `POSSIBLE` or `UNKNOWN`, never â€œno excursionâ€ from missing samples. In-band fully covered is `NO_EXCURSION_OBSERVED` scoped to window, not equipment health.

Start chain groups by accepted `correlation_id`, generator asset and outage window. Require ordered grid LOST â†’ START_REQUEST â†’ START_RESULT â†’ generator RUNNING/FAILED â†’ optional TRANSFER_RESULT â†’ bus POWERED/UNPOWERED â†’ compressor state â†’ probe response; tolerance is source-clock bound <=30 seconds for event ordering, with timeout from effective Foundation (default 5 minutes). A failed automatic start is `OBSERVED_FAILURE` only with request, explicit failed result or timeout plus complete generator state coverage through timeout, and no contradictory success; missing request/result/state is `UNKNOWN_STEP`. A transfer-position claim requires accepted transfer event; bus energization alone proves only bus state. Cooling failure cannot be assigned from temperature alone. Emit claim levels `OBSERVED`, `BOUNDED`, `POSSIBLE`, `UNKNOWN`, `CONTRADICTORY`; explanations list exact missing/excluded records and claim ceiling.

Episode key = SHA-256 of `(tenant,site,kind,subject room/generator asset,first accepted qualifying crossing/request event ID)`; retain anchor when late evidence adjusts boundaries, append `cold_episode_revision` and corresponding FindingRevision with transition `SUPPORT_CHANGED` or `BASIS_REFRESHED`. Two episodes split only after two stable in-band points (excursion) or a new start request (backup). If late evidence invalidates the anchor, append `NO_LONGER_SUPPORTED` and create a new episode with `split_from` relation; never rewrite old. Separate excursion and start-response Findings can cite the same event but share one `overlap_group` for money. Use existing S4-05 exact accepted-ID provenance and source classification (`backend/migrations/versions/0009_finding_store.py:33-87`; `.ai/DECISIONS.md:5-15`).

## 6. W2-04 consequence, reads and UI
"""Domain records for Site configuration templates and for Sites.

Two identity spaces live here and they never merge. A template has
`template_id` and `template_version`, no `site_id`, no lifecycle status, no
location, and no place-bound timezone; it cannot be listed as a Site, targeted
by a scenario, simulated, or receive evidence. A Site has `site_id` and nothing
else is its identity - not its display name, not the template it came from, and
not the shell it was created in.

Neither record type is a base, mode, or parameterization of the other, and the
Site records below repeat rather than reuse the template component shape,
because a Site's Foundation is a copy taken at creation time.

The Foundation content on both is component truth declared by a document. It is
not evidence: that a template or a Site declares a 100 kW PV array is not a
claim that any array exists, is commissioned, or has ever reported a
measurement.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass

# The M1 Site types. A template declares which kind of Site it would produce;
# that is a statement about the archetype, not about any Site.
SITE_TYPES = frozenset({"MINIGRID", "COLDCHAIN"})

# Component archetypes a template may declare. Strict and closed: an
# unsupported component type is a configuration error, not a passthrough
# string, because later slices bind topology, devices, and signal mappings to
# these values.
COMPONENT_TYPES = frozenset(
    {
        "PV_ARRAY",
        "INVERTER",
        "BATTERY",
        "POWER_CONVERSION_SYSTEM",
        "GENERATOR",
        "FUEL_TANK",
        "AC_BUS",
        "LOAD",
        "COLD_ROOM",
        "METER",
    }
)

# Units a declared design rating may use. Also closed, for the same reason:
# a canonical unit vocabulary is what later telemetry mapping is checked
# against.
RATING_UNITS = frozenset({"kW", "kWh", "kVA", "V", "A", "Hz", "L"})

# --- Typed component properties ---------------------------------------------
#
# T020A adds the second thing a Foundation may say about a component. A
# `Rating` is the one nameplate magnitude a component was sold with; a
# property is one named, typed, unit-carrying physical or control fact about
# that same component, and a component may declare several.
#
# The vocabulary below is CLOSED, in the same way `RATING_UNITS` and
# `SIGNAL_UNITS` are closed and for the same reason: a model profile binds to
# a property by name, so a document that could coin its own property keys
# could coin a key no profile will ever look for, and a run would block on a
# value that looks declared. A slice that needs a new property adds it here.
#
# The unit is the vocabulary's, not the author's. Specific fuel consumption is
# `L/kWh` because that is the machine's own property
# (`D-2026-09-22-consumption-coefficient-unit`); a document declaring it in
# `L/h` is declaring a number that is only true at one operating point, and
# the parser refuses it rather than storing it and letting a later consumer
# find out.

#: What kind of fact a property is. `PHYSICAL` is something the machine is;
#: `CONTROL` is a configured control value about it.
#:
#: `CONTROL` here is an inspectable typed property and nothing more. It is not
#: a controller, not a setpoint a run may write, and not a Site-scoped
#: Controls capability: the first controller that CONSUMES one of these
#: arrives with T024, and the T016 restriction on control-state vocabulary is
#: untouched - no switching position, no breaker state, no operating mode.
COMPONENT_PROPERTY_KINDS = frozenset({"PHYSICAL", "CONTROL"})

#: Where a declared property came from. Same two values, and the same meaning,
#: as `CONTROL_ASSUMPTION_BASES`: `TEMPLATE` is a property the archetype
#: declared and the Site copied, `SITE` is one declared for this Site.
COMPONENT_PROPERTY_SOURCES = frozenset({"TEMPLATE", "SITE"})


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class PropertyDefinition:
    """One member of the closed property vocabulary.

    The unit belongs to the definition rather than to the document: a property
    key names a quantity, and a quantity has one canonical unit here for the
    same reason a scenario parameter does.
    """

    unit: str
    kind: str
    display_name: str


#: The closed property vocabulary. Four members, which is what this slice's
#: minimum physical proof and its component-control demonstration need.
#:
#: `tank-capacity` and `specific-fuel-consumption` are the physical pair the
#: first causal kernel reads out of a frozen run. `reserve-state-of-charge`
#: and `minimum-runtime` are the control pair: typed, inspectable, and
#: consumed by nothing in this build.
COMPONENT_PROPERTY_DEFINITIONS: dict[str, PropertyDefinition] = {
    "tank-capacity": PropertyDefinition(
        unit="L", kind="PHYSICAL", display_name="Tank capacity"
    ),
    "specific-fuel-consumption": PropertyDefinition(
        unit="L/kWh",
        kind="PHYSICAL",
        display_name="Specific fuel consumption",
    ),
    "reserve-state-of-charge": PropertyDefinition(
        unit="%", kind="CONTROL", display_name="Reserve state of charge"
    ),
    "minimum-runtime": PropertyDefinition(
        unit="min", kind="CONTROL", display_name="Minimum runtime"
    ),
    # T024: the physical facts the daytime mini-grid model's laws need. The
    # model profile declares each need (Option C,
    # `D-2026-09-29-t024-option-c-and-reserve-override`) and the Foundation
    # answers it; no scenario states one of these numbers.
    "pv-rated-power": PropertyDefinition(
        unit="kW",
        kind="PHYSICAL",
        display_name="PV rated power at 1000 W/m2",
    ),
    "inverter-ac-limit": PropertyDefinition(
        unit="kW", kind="PHYSICAL", display_name="Inverter AC output limit"
    ),
    "battery-energy-capacity": PropertyDefinition(
        unit="kWh", kind="PHYSICAL", display_name="Battery energy capacity"
    ),
    "pcs-charge-limit": PropertyDefinition(
        unit="kW", kind="PHYSICAL", display_name="PCS charge power limit"
    ),
    "pcs-discharge-limit": PropertyDefinition(
        unit="kW", kind="PHYSICAL", display_name="PCS discharge power limit"
    ),
    "pcs-conversion-efficiency": PropertyDefinition(
        unit="%",
        kind="PHYSICAL",
        display_name="PCS one-way conversion efficiency",
    ),
    # T025a (`D-2026-09-29-t025-generator-policy-package`): the generator's
    # Site-owned control policy and the physical facts its lifecycle needs.
    # The `mini-grid` profile declares each as a model need and the
    # Foundation answers it; `minimum-runtime` above is now read by it.
    "start-soc-threshold": PropertyDefinition(
        unit="%", kind="CONTROL", display_name="Generator start state of charge"
    ),
    "stop-soc-threshold": PropertyDefinition(
        unit="%", kind="CONTROL", display_name="Generator stop state of charge"
    ),
    "start-delay": PropertyDefinition(
        unit="min", kind="PHYSICAL", display_name="Generator start delay"
    ),
    "generator-rated-power": PropertyDefinition(
        unit="kW", kind="PHYSICAL", display_name="Generator rated power"
    ),
}

#: Every unit a declared property may carry, derived from the vocabulary so a
#: property added above cannot introduce a unit nothing knows about and a unit
#: listed here cannot outlive the property that used it.
PROPERTY_UNITS = frozenset(
    definition.unit for definition in COMPONENT_PROPERTY_DEFINITIONS.values()
)


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ComponentProperty:
    """One typed physical or control property a Foundation declares.

    `property_key` is a member of `COMPONENT_PROPERTY_DEFINITIONS` and carries
    that definition's unit; `kind` is read from the definition rather than
    authored, because a document choosing whether its own value is physical or
    control would be a second answer to a question the vocabulary settles.

    `source` and `source_version` are provenance about this property: which
    document declared it and at which version. A property copied from a
    template keeps `TEMPLATE` and the template version it was copied at, so a
    later template change stays visible as drift rather than reaching back
    into the Site.

    Declared truth, never evidence. That a Foundation declares 0.31 L/kWh is
    not a claim that any generator has burned anything.
    """

    property_key: str
    value: float
    unit: str
    kind: str
    source: str
    source_version: int


# --- Foundation content below the component list ----------------------------
#
# T014 adds topology, devices, signal availability, device-to-signal mappings,
# and control assumptions to what a Foundation may declare. Everything here is
# shared between a template's Foundation and a Site's Foundation, in the same
# way `Rating` already is: these are leaf value types, and a Site's copy is
# free to diverge in its VALUES without either Foundation growing a mode.
#
# Two vocabularies this slice deliberately does NOT settle, because the T016
# user-review checkpoint owns them:
#
#   - whether a breaker is a device, component state, or both, and what the
#     control state vocabulary is. `ControlAssumption` below therefore carries
#     a declared statement and its basis, not a control model.
#   - how cold-room process symbols relate to mini-grid electrical topology.
#     `TOPOLOGY_NODE_ROLES` carries only the electrical positions the shipped
#     hybrid mini-grid template needs to parse.

# Where a component sits in the site's electrical topology. Closed, because a
# later SLD archetype binds against these and an unrecognised role would be a
# node nothing could draw.
#
# This is topology position, not component identity: `component_type` remains
# the canonical vocabulary for what a component IS, and no component carries a
# second role axis.
#
# `GENERATION` rather than `SOURCE` deliberately. `source` already means where
# a Site's evidence comes from, and a topology role sharing that word would put
# two unrelated meanings of it on one screen.
TOPOLOGY_NODE_ROLES = frozenset(
    {
        "GENERATION",
        "CONVERSION",
        "STORAGE",
        "BUS",
        "METERING",
        "LOAD",
        "FUEL_STORAGE",
    }
)

# What flows along a connection. Closed for the same reason: a connection whose
# medium is not understood cannot be drawn or simulated, so it is a
# configuration error rather than a passthrough string.
CONNECTION_MEDIA = frozenset({"AC", "DC", "FUEL"})

# What kind of thing a declared device is. Deliberately small: only what the
# shipped hybrid mini-grid template needs. A device here is a configured asset,
# never an operating one - nothing in this record says a device exists, has
# been commissioned, or has ever reported.
DEVICE_TYPES = frozenset({"METER", "SENSOR", "CONTROLLER"})

# Units a declared signal may carry. Closed, and separate from `RATING_UNITS`:
# a nameplate rating and a reportable signal are different kinds of fact, and
# collapsing their vocabularies would let a rating unit onto a signal.
#
# T025b adds `state`: a signal whose reading is a typed discrete member (a
# generator's `RUNNING`), never a number. It is the unit the execution contract
# already types discrete values by (`assetops_contracts.control.DISCRETE_UNIT`).
SIGNAL_UNITS = frozenset(
    {"kW", "kWh", "V", "Hz", "L", "degC", "W/m2", "%", "state"}
)

# --- Typed Site Controls (T024) ----------------------------------------------
#
# The Site-scoped half of v4 section 5.2's typed control properties. A
# component control (a battery's reserve) is a component property; a control
# about the installation as a whole - which source serves load first, which
# load is protected first - has no component to hang on, so it lives in its
# own collection rather than abusing the property table.
#
# `ControlAssumption` stays documentary and is untouched: an assumption says
# in words how a site is meant to run, and a Site Control is a typed value a
# controller consumes. Both may describe the same intent; only one is read.
#
# Time validity is the containing Foundation's: a Site Control is valid from
# the Foundation's `valid_from` at its `version`, and a run freezes both.
#
# Priority lists name explicit component identities. The parser requires each
# list to name EVERY component of its member types exactly once, so there is
# no implicit tie order and no display-name matching.


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class SiteControlDefinition:
    """One member of the closed Site Control vocabulary."""

    display_name: str
"""The code-owned MINIGRID signal role vocabulary (S1-02).

Decided by `D-2026-10-08-s1-bindings-roles-and-snapshot` (Architect answers
Q1, Q3 and Q4 in `.agent/architect-S1-02-answers-2026-10-08.md`). Every
vocabulary choice lives here; the binding mechanism does not name a role.

A role names what a bound channel means for analysis. The canonical unit is
fixed per role. A role is either a channel role (bound to one registered
device signal) or a configuration role (covered by a Foundation control, not
by a channel).
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass

#: S1-02's vocabulary. Kept readable for every binding set recorded under it.
ROLE_SET_MINIGRID_1 = "minigrid-roles-1"
#: S3-01 (`D-2026-10-11-s3-dispatch-interval-assessment`, Architect batch 5
#: "S3-01 Planner questions"): roles-1 plus the two optional capability
#: channels a dispatch Candidate needs. Observed `pv_power` and
#: `battery_power` are not these capabilities.
ROLE_SET_MINIGRID_2 = "minigrid-roles-2"
#: S6-02a (`D-2026-10-16-s6-verification`, `D-2026-10-16-s602-questions`):
#: roles-2 plus the requested and delivered critical-load power a reserve
#: trial's service guardrail needs. A Site opts in by publishing with this
#: role set explicitly; new publications still default to roles-2.
ROLE_SET_MINIGRID_3 = "minigrid-roles-3"
#: The set a new MINIGRID binding publication is recorded under.
ROLE_SET_MINIGRID = ROLE_SET_MINIGRID_2

#: The canonical generator states an ENUM conversion may map to.
GENERATOR_STATES: tuple[str, ...] = ("STOPPED", "STARTING", "RUNNING", "FAILED")

#: One binding per Site, or one per asset of the role's asset type.
ONE = "ONE"
PER_ASSET = "PER_ASSET"


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Role:
    role: str
    label: str
    asset_type: str
    canonical_unit: str
    value_kind: str  # NUMBER | STATE
    cardinality: str  # ONE | PER_ASSET
    required: bool
    #: "CHANNEL" roles are bound to a device signal; "FOUNDATION_CONTROL"
    #: roles are covered by a configured Foundation control (decision Q3).
    covered_by: str = "CHANNEL"
    #: S6-02a: a ONE role describing the Site's critical load - the first
    #: member of the Foundation's `critical-load-priority` control. The
    #: control identifies the one asset, so several LOAD components are not
    #: an ambiguity; a binding naming any other load is refused.
    critical_load: bool = False


MINIGRID_ROLES: tuple[Role, ...] = (
    Role("generator_state", "Generator state", "GENERATOR", "state", "STATE", ONE, True),
    Role("generator_power", "Generator power", "GENERATOR", "kW", "NUMBER", ONE, True),
    Role("feeder_demand", "Feeder demand", "LOAD", "kW", "NUMBER", PER_ASSET, True),
    Role("battery_soc", "Battery state of charge", "BATTERY", "%", "NUMBER", ONE, True),
    Role(
        "battery_reserve", "Battery reserve", "BATTERY", "%", "NUMBER", ONE, True,
        covered_by="FOUNDATION_CONTROL",
    ),
    Role("pv_power", "Solar inverter power", "INVERTER", "kW", "NUMBER", ONE, False),
    Role("pv_irradiance", "Solar irradiance", "PV_ARRAY", "W/m2", "NUMBER", ONE, False),
    Role("battery_power", "Battery converter power", "POWER_CONVERSION_SYSTEM", "kW", "NUMBER", ONE, False),
    Role("fuel_level", "Fuel level", "FUEL_TANK", "L", "NUMBER", ONE, False),
)

MINIGRID_ROLES_2: tuple[Role, ...] = MINIGRID_ROLES + (
    Role("pv_available_power", "Solar available power", "INVERTER", "kW", "NUMBER", ONE, False),
    Role(
        "pcs_discharge_capability", "Battery converter discharge capability",
        "POWER_CONVERSION_SYSTEM", "kW", "NUMBER", ONE, False,
    ),
)

MINIGRID_ROLES_3: tuple[Role, ...] = MINIGRID_ROLES_2 + (
    Role(
        "critical_load_requested_power", "Critical load requested power", "LOAD", "kW", "NUMBER", ONE, False,
        critical_load=True,
    ),
    Role(
        "critical_load_delivered_power", "Critical load delivered power", "LOAD", "kW", "NUMBER", ONE, False,
        critical_load=True,
    ),
)

ROLES_BY_SET: dict[str, tuple[Role, ...]] = {
    ROLE_SET_MINIGRID_1: MINIGRID_ROLES,
    ROLE_SET_MINIGRID_2: MINIGRID_ROLES_2,
    ROLE_SET_MINIGRID_3: MINIGRID_ROLES_3,
}
#: The role set a new publication for a Site type is recorded under.
ROLE_SET_BY_SITE_TYPE: dict[str, str] = {"MINIGRID": ROLE_SET_MINIGRID}
#: Every role set a Site type still serves: a binding set recorded under an
#: older version stays interpretable (and selectable as a historical basis)
#: under the role definitions it was published with, never today's.
SERVED_ROLE_SETS_BY_SITE_TYPE: dict[str, frozenset[str]] = {
    "MINIGRID": frozenset({ROLE_SET_MINIGRID_1, ROLE_SET_MINIGRID_2, ROLE_SET_MINIGRID_3}),
}

#: S6-02a verification inputs (Architect S6-02a Q2). Interval-power roles
#: need five-minute interval-valued readings covering each UTC slot; point
#: roles need an accepted sample at each five-minute boundary.
VERIFICATION_ROLE_SETS: frozenset[str] = frozenset({ROLE_SET_MINIGRID_3})
VERIFICATION_POWER_ROLES: tuple[str, ...] = (
    "generator_power", "feeder_demand", "pv_available_power", "pcs_discharge_capability",
    "critical_load_requested_power", "critical_load_delivered_power",
)
VERIFICATION_POINT_ROLES: tuple[str, ...] = ("generator_state", "battery_soc")
#: Names the wording below; any wording change is a new version.
VERIFICATION_READINESS_VERSION = "verification-readiness-1"

_LAB_INTERVAL = (
    "Run the Lab with the five-minute publication profile (registered-kobo-publication v4), "
    "which publishes this channel as an interval mean for every five-minute slot."
)
_LAB_POINT = (
    "Run the Lab with the five-minute publication profile (registered-kobo-publication v4), "
    "which publishes a sample at every five-minute boundary."
)
_SITE_INTERVAL = (
    "Publish five-minute interval-valued power in kW: an interval mean whose interval covers "
    "each UTC five-minute slot, or non-overlapping sub-intervals that exactly partition it. "
    "Point readings alone do not count."
)
#: role -> (Lab message, real-Site message).
VERIFICATION_MESSAGES: dict[str, tuple[str, str]] = {
    "generator_power": (_LAB_INTERVAL, "Bind the generator output meter. " + _SITE_INTERVAL),
    "feeder_demand": (_LAB_INTERVAL, "Bind a feeder meter for every load asset. " + _SITE_INTERVAL),
    "pv_available_power": (
        _LAB_INTERVAL, "Bind the inverter's available (uncurtailed) power. " + _SITE_INTERVAL,
    ),
    "pcs_discharge_capability": (
        _LAB_INTERVAL, "Bind the battery converter's discharge capability. " + _SITE_INTERVAL,
    ),
    "critical_load_requested_power": (
        _LAB_INTERVAL, "Bind the power the critical load requested. " + _SITE_INTERVAL,
    ),
    "critical_load_delivered_power": (
        _LAB_INTERVAL, "Bind the power delivered to the critical load. " + _SITE_INTERVAL,
    ),
    "generator_state": (
        _LAB_POINT, "Bind the generator state and publish a sample at every UTC five-minute boundary.",
    ),
    "battery_soc": (
        _LAB_POINT,
        "Bind battery state of charge (%) and publish a sample at every UTC five-minute boundary, "
        "including the window end.",
    ),
}


def serves(site_type: str, role_set: str) -> bool:
    """Whether a Site of `site_type` can read bindings recorded under `role_set`."""
    return role_set in SERVED_ROLE_SETS_BY_SITE_TYPE.get(site_type, frozenset())

#: S1 publishes numeric IDENTITY and generator-state ENUM only (decision Q4).
#: LINEAR is deferred until supported unit pairs, coefficients and
#: normalization rules are decided; a LINEAR conversion is refused.
CONVERSION_KINDS = ("IDENTITY", "ENUM")
DEFERRED_CONVERSION_KINDS = ("LINEAR",)


def roles_for(role_set: str) -> dict[str, Role]:
    return {role.role: role for role in ROLES_BY_SET.get(role_set, ())}


def conversion_problem(role: Role, source_unit: str, conversion: object) -> str | None:
    """Why this conversion cannot turn `source_unit` into the role's unit, or None."""
    if not isinstance(conversion, dict) or set(conversion) != {"kind", "parameters"}:
        return "conversion must be an object with exactly 'kind' and 'parameters'"
    kind, parameters = conversion["kind"], conversion["parameters"]
    if kind in DEFERRED_CONVERSION_KINDS:
        return f"a {kind} conversion is not published in this release; only IDENTITY and ENUM are"
    if kind not in CONVERSION_KINDS:
        return f"conversion kind {kind!r} is not one of {', '.join(CONVERSION_KINDS)}"
    if not isinstance(parameters, dict):
        return "conversion parameters must be an object"
    if kind == "IDENTITY":
        if parameters:
            return "an IDENTITY conversion takes no parameters"
        if role.value_kind != "NUMBER":
            return f"{role.label} is a state; use an ENUM conversion"
        if source_unit != role.canonical_unit:
            return (
                f"an IDENTITY conversion needs the source unit to be {role.canonical_unit!r}, "
                f"not {source_unit!r}"
            )
        return None
    # ENUM
    if role.value_kind != "STATE":
        return f"{role.label} is a number; an ENUM conversion applies only to states"
    if set(parameters) != {"map"} or not isinstance(parameters["map"], dict) or not parameters["map"]:
        return "an ENUM conversion takes exactly a non-empty 'map'"
    for label, state in parameters["map"].items():
        if not isinstance(label, str) or state not in GENERATOR_STATES:
            return f"ENUM map entry {label!r} must map to one of {', '.join(GENERATOR_STATES)}"
    if source_unit != role.canonical_unit:
        return f"a state is reported in {role.canonical_unit!r}, not {source_unit!r}"
    return None

frontend/docs/architecture\production-shaped-mvp-technical-design.md:43:`gateway_diagnostic` is the central operational-health table, separate from `accepted_evidence`: `id`, `tenant_id`, `site_id`, `gateway_id`, `sequence_epoch`, `diagnostic_sequence`, `observed_at`, `received_at`, `last_device_contact_by_source jsonb`, `queue_count`, `oldest_queue_age_seconds?`, `disk_pressure`, `clock_offset_ms?`, `mapping_error_count`, `payload_sha256`. Use a same-Site gateway FK, `UNIQUE(gateway_id,sequence_epoch,diagnostic_sequence)` and indexes `(site_id,received_at DESC)` and `(gateway_id,received_at DESC)`. Same identity/hash is an idempotent retry; changed bytes with the same identity are refused and audited. Rows are append-only. The server, not the gateway clock, sets `received_at` and computes current contact freshness from a declared heartbeat cadence. A stale/missing report never becomes a healthy-device assertion. This table and `GatewayDiagnosticsV1` are the recorded `A-edge-diagnostics` contract; S2-01c/S2-02 refine cadence and limits.

frontend/docs/architecture\production-shaped-mvp-technical-design.md:185:**Gateway diagnostic API:** `POST /api/gateways/{gateway_id}/diagnostics` authenticates the registered gateway/site, checks `GatewayDiagnosticsV1` identity/hash and inserts `gateway_diagnostic` with server `received_at`; an identical retry returns the existing row. `409` refuses identity reuse with different bytes, `422` malformed fields, and `401/403` wrong credential/scope. `GET /api/sites/{id}/gateway-health` returns last server contact, declared cadence, source-contact observations, queue age/depth, disk pressure, clock offset and an explicit `CURRENT|STALE|UNKNOWN` status with diagnostic ID and receive time. It makes no claim that the device or asset itself is healthy. S2-01c publishes locally; S2-02 owns central storage and read API; S7 composes the Site Health claim.

/**
 * The approved v69 panel title map (`D-2026-10-17-mockup-layout-spec`,
 * `.agent/architect-W1-fidelity.md`). It is exhaustive: every screen's expected
 * panel titles are exactly `frontend/docs/architecture/v69-rendered-panels.json`,
 * except the five substitutions below, which the Architect approved. Do not add
 * an entry to keep an extra app panel; move its content into its owning panel.
 *
 * A dynamic title is a function of the selected read, never of a mock fixture.
 */
import rendered from "../../docs/architecture/v69-rendered-panels.json";

export type DynamicTitle = (values: Record<string, string>) => string;

/** `screen:index` â†’ the approved replacement for the JSON title. */
export const APPROVED_TITLE_CHANGES: Readonly<Record<string, string | DynamicTitle>> = {
  "foundation:2": "Identity and operating obligations",
  "dispatch:2": (v) => `Selected block Â· ${need(v, "interval")}`,
  "readiness:2": (v) => `Gateway status Â· ${need(v, "gateway")}`,
  "finding-evidence:1": (v) => `Evidence bundle Â· ${need(v, "finding")}`,
  "sld:1": (v) => (v.topology ? `${need(v, "site")} Â· ${v.topology}` : need(v, "site")),
};

function need(values: Record<string, string>, key: string): string {
  const value = values[key];
  if (value === undefined || value === "") {
    throw new Error(`The dynamic panel title needs the selected read's "${key}" value.`);
  }
  return value;
}

const PANELS = (rendered as { panels: Record<string, { index: number; title: string }[]> }).panels;

/** The expected panel titles of one v69 screen, in order. */
export function expectedPanelTitles(screenId: string, values: Record<string, string> = {}): string[] {
  const entry = PANELS[screenId];
  if (entry === undefined) throw new Error(`No v69 rendered-panel entry for screen "${screenId}".`);
  return [...entry]
    .sort((a, b) => a.index - b.index)
    .map(({ index, title }) => {
      const change = APPROVED_TITLE_CHANGES[`${screenId}:${index}`];
      if (change === undefined) return title;
      return typeof change === "string" ? change : change(values);
    });
}
"""Versioned model and publication profiles, and what they resolve.

A profile is versioned simulator configuration. It is not authored scenario
content and it is not Site configuration: it says what a simulator build can
model and how a simulated site publishes, which is the same for every scenario
and every Site that build runs.

Two profiles, selected separately by run setup, because they answer two
different questions:

- a **model profile** says which private world states this simulator can
  model, and in which execution roles. `EXECUTION_REQUIREMENTS` gives an input
  two values and no third, so a required input this profile does not support
  blocks the run and an optional one is recorded as unsupported. There is no
  "supported if convenient", which is how an input gets ignored.
- a **publication profile** says at what cadence a device signal reports, which
  simulator source and which gateway a run would publish through, and - since
  T022 - which configured device signals report which world state, with what
  error and with what reliability. That last one is what the observation
  transform consumes, and it is here because the publication profile owns
  reporting behaviour: a Foundation says a signal CAN report and declares its
  unit, and declares no rate, no bias and no dropout.

## Why this module may not see a Site

`D-2026-09-21-scenario-execution-contract` settles that Foundation declares
that a signal CAN report and declares no rate, and that nothing derives a
cadence from a device's name, from displayed text, or from the spacing between
authored rows. T019 carries the same rule for the two publication identities:
they come from the selected profile or the run is `BLOCKED`.

The rule is structural here rather than remembered. This module imports
nothing from `assetops_backend.sites`, the three resolver functions below take
only a profile and the scenario's own declared source, and
`tools/checks/run-setup.ps1` fails the build if a `FrozenObservationBinding` or
a `FrozenPublicationIdentity` is constructed anywhere else in the product. A
Site fact cannot reach a cadence, because it cannot reach this module and
nothing outside it may build the record a cadence lives in.

## What the shipped profiles say, and what they deliberately do not

`MINIMAL_FUEL_TANK_MODEL` is the profile the first causal kernel will
implement. It models the fuel tank, the generator's own consumption
coefficient and the output the scenario forces on it, and nothing else - which
is the truth about this build. The shipped Fuel Loss Event also declares site
demand and plane-of-array irradiance, and this profile supports neither.

What changed in T020B is what that costs, and it is worth being exact because
the cost used to be a blocked run. Those two states are now declared
`OPTIONAL` in that document, because producing the fuel trajectory does not
need either - the dispatch is declared directly, so nothing computes it from
load and PV (`D-2026-09-22-forcing-state-requirements`). An `OPTIONAL` state
this profile does not model is RECORDED on the run as an unsupported optional
input, named on the run's own screen, and does not block it. Widening the
profile to model them is T024's work, and it widens by modelling a state
rather than by declaring that it does.

The third state that used to block that document was never this profile's to
answer. Whether the fuel level sensor is reporting is a fact about the
reporting path, so `REPORTING_PATH_STATES` names it and the publication
profile declares whether it can be modelled. A model profile that claimed it
is refused.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass, replace

from assetops_backend.runs.models import (
    FrozenObservationBinding,
    FrozenPublicationIdentity,
    FrozenSignalResolution,
)
from assetops_backend.scenarios.models import ObservationSource
from assetops_backend.state_refs import STATE_SCOPES
from assetops_contracts.control import DISCRETE_UNIT
from assetops_contracts.observation import (
    REPORTABLE_READING_CLASSES,
    device_resolution,
    refuse_numeric_error_on_a_discrete_signal,
)


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class FoundationBinding:
    """How a Foundation-owned initial world value is found in a Foundation.

    The scenario says the Foundation owns a value; this says which declared
    fact answers for it. Declared rather than guessed: nothing matches a state
    key against a component identity by spelling, for the same reason T018's
    review made a bound a declaration rather than a shared prefix.

    Three fields since T020A, and the middle one is the slice. It used to name
    a component type and a RATING unit, which can address exactly one fact per
    component: a generator has one rating, so a profile needing both its
    specific fuel consumption and its minimum runtime had nothing to say which
    it meant. `property_key` names the typed property, so the binding aims at a
    named fact rather than at whatever the component happened to be rated in.

    That is also why a Foundation declaring no such property BLOCKS rather than
    refusing (`D-2026-09-22-foundation-property-absent-blocks`): the property
    name is as much this profile's aim as the component type is, and a
    different profile naming a different property may well find something the
    same Foundation does declare.

    This module names three strings and reads no Foundation. Resolving the
    binding against a Site is the run setup service's job, which is the same
    parser/service split the target-site declaration uses - and it is enforced,
    because `tools/checks/run-setup.ps1` forbids this module from importing the
    Site record family at all.

    ## Still three strings after T020A1, and deliberately no fourth

    Addressing did not add a component id here. A binding says WHAT KIND of
    declared fact answers - a `FUEL_TANK`'s `tank-capacity`, in litres - and
    the scenario says WHICH ONE, because the scenario is the thing that knows
    the installation it targets. A component id in a profile would make the
    profile a fixture: the same simulator build could not be selected for a
    second Site without being edited, which is what acceptance criterion 3
    asks not to happen. What addressing did add is one field on
    `SupportedState`, at the same grain: a kind of claim, never an instance.
    """

    component_type: str
    property_key: str
    unit: str


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class SupportedState:
    """One private world state a model profile can model, and how.

    `supported_roles` is the set of execution roles this profile can consume
    for this state. A state it can cause but not report is a real and common
    case - a kernel can move a tank level long before anything publishes a
    reading of it - so the two are separate rather than one flag.

    ## `scope` is a fourth field since T020A1, and it names no fixture

    It says whether this state is a fact about ONE COMPONENT or about the
    installation. Stored fuel volume is a component's; plane-of-array
    irradiance is the site's. That is a property of the state the simulator
    models, not of any installation, which is why it is declared here and why
    it can be declared without naming a single component id - the point of
    acceptance criterion 3. Select this same profile for a different Site and
    it addresses that Site's components.

    A scenario declares the scope too, on each reference, and run setup blocks
    when they disagree. Two declarations of one fact sounds like a place for
    them to drift, and the disagreement is the substance: the scenario says
    which instance it means and the profile says whether instances are a thing
    this state has. A scenario addressing site-wide irradiance at one PV array
    has asked for something the model does not carry, and saying so is more
    use than quietly answering the question it did not ask.

    A `SITE` state has no `foundation_binding`, refused below rather than
    remembered. A binding names a component type and a property on it, which
    is a component-scoped answer by construction; hanging one on a site-wide
    state would be a declaration that could never resolve.
    """

    state_key: str
    scope: str
    supported_roles: frozenset[str]
    foundation_binding: FoundationBinding | None
    statement: str
    #: T024. The kind of component a COMPONENT state with no Foundation
    #: binding is carried on - a load's demand is a fact about a LOAD. Without
    #: it an explicitly addressed reference could name any component at all,
    #: because the binding's component type was the only type check there was.
        _reading(
            "generator-controller",
            "status",
            "STATE_AT_INSTANT",
            "state",
            GENERATOR_STATUS_MEMBERS,
        ),
        _reading("battery-pcs-controller", "state-of-charge", "STATE_AT_INSTANT", "%"),
        _reading("battery-pcs-controller", "ac-power", "INTERVAL_MEAN", "kW"),
        _reading(
            "battery-pcs-controller", "discharge-capability", "INTERVAL_MEAN", "kW"
        ),
        _reading("pv-inverter-controller", "ac-power", "INTERVAL_MEAN", "kW"),
        _reading("pv-inverter-controller", "available-power", "INTERVAL_MEAN", "kW"),
        _reading("residential-meter", "active-power", "INTERVAL_MEAN", "kW"),
        _reading("clinic-meter", "active-power", "INTERVAL_MEAN", "kW"),
        _reading("mill-meter", "active-power", "INTERVAL_MEAN", "kW"),
        _record("generator-controller", "policy-start-soc-threshold", "POLICY", "%"),
        _record("generator-controller", "policy-stop-soc-threshold", "POLICY", "%"),
        _record("generator-controller", "policy-minimum-runtime", "POLICY", "min"),
        _record("battery-pcs-controller", "policy-reserve-soc", "POLICY", "%"),
        _record("site-controller", "policy-source-priority", "POLICY", "order"),
        _record(
            "site-controller", "policy-critical-load-priority", "POLICY", "order"
        ),
        _record(
            "generator-controller", "override-command", "OPERATIONAL_RECORD", "%"
        ),
        _record(
            "battery-pcs-controller", "override-command", "OPERATIONAL_RECORD", "%"
        ),
    ),
)

#: Which frozen mapping a run publishes through, by the publication profile it
#: froze. A profile not listed publishes through the fuel-only mapping, which
#: is what every Draft frozen before T026 did.
_MAPPING_BY_PUBLICATION_PROFILE = {
    ("simulator-lab-publication", 5): SIMULATOR_LAB_DISPATCH_MAPPINGS,
}


def gateway_mapping_for(

        (publication_profile_id, publication_profile_version),
        SIMULATOR_LAB_GATEWAY_MAPPINGS,

                for control in controls

# --- Topology ---------------------------------------------------------------


def _parse_topology(
    raw: Any,
    *,
    component_ids: frozenset[str],
    where: str,
    source: str,
    invalid: Invalid,
) -> FoundationTopology | None:
    if raw is None:
        return None

    if not isinstance(raw, Mapping):
        invalid(
            f"'{where}' must be an object declaring nodes and connections, or "
            f"be absent, in {source}. An absent topology is the document "
            "declaring none; it is not a topology with nothing in it."
        )

    _reject_unknown_keys(raw, TOPOLOGY_KEYS, where=where, source=source, invalid=invalid)

    raw_nodes = _require_non_empty_list(
        raw.get("nodes"),
        where=f"{where}.nodes",
        source=source,
        limit=MAX_TOPOLOGY_NODES,
        invalid=invalid,
    )
    nodes = tuple(
        _parse_topology_node(
            entry,
            component_ids=component_ids,
            where=f"{where}.nodes[{index}]",
            source=source,
            invalid=invalid,
        )
        for index, entry in enumerate(raw_nodes)
    )
    _reject_duplicates(
        [node.node_id for node in nodes],
        kind="node_id",
        where=f"{where}.nodes",
        source=source,
        invalid=invalid,
    )

    # One node per component. Two nodes for one component would make the
    # topology ambiguous about where that component sits, and a later diagram
    # would have to pick one.
    _reject_duplicates(
        [node.component_id for node in nodes],
        kind="component_id",
        where=f"{where}.nodes",
        source=source,
        invalid=invalid,
    )

    node_ids = frozenset(node.node_id for node in nodes)

    raw_connections = _require_non_empty_list(
        raw.get("connections"),
        where=f"{where}.connections",
        source=source,
        limit=MAX_CONNECTIONS,
        invalid=invalid,
    )
    connections = tuple(
        _parse_connection(
            entry,
            node_ids=node_ids,
            where=f"{where}.connections[{index}]",
            source=source,
            invalid=invalid,
        )
        for index, entry in enumerate(raw_connections)
    )
    _reject_duplicates(
        [connection.connection_id for connection in connections],
        kind="connection_id",
        where=f"{where}.connections",
        source=source,
        invalid=invalid,
    )

    return FoundationTopology(nodes=nodes, connections=connections)


def _parse_topology_node(
    raw: Any,
    *,
    component_ids: frozenset[str],
    site: StoredSite,
    role_set: str,
    requests: Sequence[BindingRequest],
    registrations: Registrations,
    foundation: Sequence[RevisionRow],
) -> tuple[BindingRow, ...]:
    """Every request as a binding row, or a refusal naming every problem.

    `foundation` is every currently recorded Foundation revision the binding
    period overlaps (after any joint Foundation change); each must declare the
    channel with the source unit.
    """
    problems: list[dict[str, str]] = []

    def problem(code: str, index: int | None, message: str) -> None:
        problems.append({"code": code, "binding": "" if index is None else str(index), "message": message})

    roles = roles_for(role_set)
    if not roles:
        problem("BINDING_ROLE_SET_UNKNOWN", None, f"Role set {role_set!r} is not defined.")
    gateways = registrations.gateways
    if len(gateways) != 1:
        problem("BINDING_GATEWAY_UNKNOWN", None, f"{site.site_code} must have exactly one registered gateway.")
    assets = {str(a.id): a for a in registrations.assets}
    sources = {
        s.source_external_id: s
        for s in registrations.sources
        if gateways and s.gateway_id == gateways[0].id
    }
    declared = [(_declared_signals(row.content), row) for row in foundation]
    if not foundation:
        problem(
            "BINDING_FOUNDATION_MISSING", None,
            "No currently recorded Foundation period covers this binding period.",
        )

    # ONE means one per Site and never a choice among several (decision Q1,
    # review M2): a Foundation period with more than one eligible component for
    # a ONE role it needs is refused, not resolved by picking one.
    requested_roles = {req.role for req in requests}
    for row in foundation:
        components = list(row.content.get("components") or ())
        for role in roles.values():
            if role.cardinality != "ONE" or not (role.required or role.role in requested_roles):
                continue
            if role.critical_load:
                continue  # the critical-load-priority control names the one asset
            eligible = sorted(c["component_id"] for c in components if c.get("component_type") == role.asset_type)
            if len(eligible) > 1:
                problem(
                    "BINDING_ROLE_AMBIGUOUS", None,
                    f"{role.label} is one per Site in {role_set}, but Foundation v{row.version} declares "
                    f"{len(eligible)} {role.asset_type} components ({', '.join(eligible)}); this release "
                    "does not choose among them.",
                )

    rows: list[BindingRow] = []
    channels: set[tuple[str, str]] = set()
    single: dict[tuple[str, str | None], int] = {}
    for index, req in enumerate(requests):
        channel = (req.device_id, req.signal_id)
        name = f"{req.device_id} / {req.signal_id}"
        if channel in channels:
            problem("BINDING_CHANNEL_OVERLAP", index, f"Channel {name} is bound more than once in this set.")
        channels.add(channel)
        source = sources.get(req.device_id)
        if source is None:
            problem(
                "BINDING_SOURCE_UNKNOWN", index,
                f"Device {req.device_id!r} is not a registered source of {site.site_code}'s gateway.",
            )
        for signals, row in declared:
            unit = signals.get(channel)
            if unit is None:
                problem(
                    "BINDING_SIGNAL_UNDECLARED", index,
                    f"Foundation v{row.version} declares no signal {name}.",
                )
            elif unit != req.source_unit:
                problem(
                    "BINDING_UNIT_MISMATCH", index,
                    f"{name} is declared in {unit!r} in Foundation v{row.version}, not {req.source_unit!r}.",
                )
        asset = None
        if req.asset_id is not None:
            asset = assets.get(req.asset_id)
            if asset is None:
                problem(
                    "BINDING_ASSET_UNKNOWN", index,
                    f"Asset {req.asset_id} is not a registered asset of {site.site_code}.",
                )
        role: Role | None = roles.get(req.role)
        if role is None:
            problem("BINDING_ROLE_UNKNOWN", index, f"Role {req.role!r} is not in {role_set}.")
        elif role.covered_by != "CHANNEL":
            problem(
                "BINDING_ROLE_NOT_A_CHANNEL", index,
                f"{role.label} is covered by a Foundation control, not by a channel binding.",
            )
        else:
            if req.canonical_unit != role.canonical_unit:
                problem(
                    "BINDING_UNIT_MISMATCH", index,
                    f"{role.label} is stated in {role.canonical_unit!r}, not {req.canonical_unit!r}.",
                )
            why = conversion_problem(role, req.source_unit, req.conversion)
            if why is not None:
                problem("BINDING_CONVERSION_INVALID", index, f"{name}: {why}.")
            if req.asset_id is None:
                problem("BINDING_ASSET_REQUIRED", index, f"{role.label} must name the asset it describes.")
            elif asset is not None and asset.asset_type != role.asset_type:
                problem(
                    "BINDING_ROLE_ASSET_MISMATCH", index,
                    f"{role.label} describes a {role.asset_type} asset; {asset.asset_code} is {asset.asset_type}.",
                )
            if role.critical_load and asset is not None:
                for row in foundation:
                    first = critical_load_code(row.content)
                    if first != asset.asset_code:
                        problem(
                            "BINDING_CRITICAL_LOAD_MISMATCH", index,
                            f"{role.label} must name the first load of the critical-load priority in Foundation "
                            f"v{row.version} ({first or 'not configured'}), not {asset.asset_code}.",
                        )
            if role.role == "battery_soc" and asset is not None:
                # The state of charge and the reserve control must describe one battery.
                for row in foundation:
                    reserve = battery_reserve(row.content)
                    if reserve is not None and reserve["asset_code"] != asset.asset_code:
                        problem(
                            "BINDING_BATTERY_MISMATCH", index,
                            f"{role.label} names {asset.asset_code}, but the battery reserve in Foundation "
                            f"v{row.version} is configured on {reserve['asset_code']}.",
                        )
            key = (role.role, req.asset_id if role.cardinality == "PER_ASSET" else None)
            if key in single:
                problem(
                    "BINDING_ROLE_CONFLICT", index,
                    f"{role.label} is already bound by bindings[{single[key]}]"
                    + (" for this asset." if key[1] else "."),
                )
            single.setdefault(key, index)
        if source is not None and gateways:
            rows.append(
                BindingRow(
                    id=uuid4(),
                    gateway_id=gateways[0].id,
                    source_id=source.id,
                    device_id=req.device_id,
                    signal_id=req.signal_id,
                    asset_id=None if asset is None else asset.id,
                    role=req.role,
                    source_unit=req.source_unit,
                    canonical_unit=req.canonical_unit,
                    conversion=req.conversion,
                )
            )
    if problems:
        first = problems[0]
        raise BindingRefused(
            first["code"],

## 2026-10-17

Decision: `D-2026-10-17-w2-00-answers`. Codex Architect resolves the eight W2-00 phase-1 questions in `.agent/W2-technical-design-cold.md` Â§1 and `tasks/W2-00-source-events.md`. Event equality hashes canonical source, source-time/interval timing and full payload, excluding transport/mapping/publication fields; same-message comparison keeps full-envelope SHA and wins collision precedence. Migration `0020` keys delta constraints on `measurement='INTERVAL_DELTA'`, leaving `conversion_kind` as the binding conversion. Batch V2 keeps ordinary v3 strict while a valid v4 or v3-v4-only header reaches per-ordinal parser refusal; an unusable header or cross-tenant binding selection remains a whole-batch refusal. Parser and S2 own the exact EV/correlation codes; five optional snapshot-binding keys and a closed v4 channel table define mapping compatibility. W2-00 may prove v4 with test-only injected registered binding bases; W2-01 publishes cold bindings, W2-02 registers/emits the cold runtime profile, and W2-05b registers/emits EV, an explicit W2-00 producer/profile deviation deferred to W2-02. V4 acceptance queues no dispatch work until its domain worker is introduced. The new family/kind read applies to Postgres; the legacy file route rejects those filters. This is design authority, not code, review verdict, registry promotion or owner approval.

## 2026-10-16

Decision: `D-2026-10-16-s408`. Codex Architect accepts S4-08 Q1-Q3 with the display rule in `.agent/architect-S4-08-answers.md`. The Site consequence history includes unpriced versions with typed missing-price/null amount. `CURRENT_AT_CUTOFF` identifies only the latest consequence within the latest FindingRevision recorded by that cutoff; if that FindingRevision has no consequence, no row for that Finding is current at cutoff. A selected past cutoff is audit history: an eligible recorded amount is labelled historical and assumption-conditioned, never a current-money claim. Exact-version source and existing money gates still govern display, and a closed source hides amounts. Only today's separate exact-head read may claim current money after all gates pass. This implements the Site-history requirement of `D-2026-10-14-s402-post-review-follow-ons` without promoting `assumptions:3#a` or recording owner acceptance.

Decision: `D-2026-10-16-w1-ux-disclosure`. Codex Architect accepts the W1-UX-01 narrow disclosure scan as an amendment to `D-2026-10-04-plain-language-analysis-views`, with the exact banned disclosure-prose terms, permitted individually labelled references, and case-sensitive raw-code detection defined in `.agent/architect-W1-ux-disclosure-answers.md`. The closed disclosure is scanned, never exempted as a container; the existing single-value exemption rule stays. Raw IDs, versions, digests and reasons may appear only as labelled exact values, never in the collapsed summary. Raw-code matching does not flag ordinary units or acronyms including SOC, UTC, kW, kWh and PV. Plain state, material limits, scope and as-of time stay visible outside the disclosure. This does not record owner acceptance or change the W1 walkthrough gate.

Decision: `D-2026-10-16-w1-ux`. Codex Architect. For W1 product surfaces, substantive deeper analysis has a separate contextual destination, following the owner-directed T028 Analysis tabs. Compact cards and indexes link to their owning detail; a closed inline "How this was worked out" disclosure holds only small exact-reference detail and is not the sole route to method or evidence. Keep the same destination at 1440 and 390 px. Approve Site, Evidence, Finding, Action, Verification and Analysis as explained domain/navigation labels; ban raw states, codes, IDs, implementation analytics and simulator vocabulary from the default reading, with narrowly labelled exact-reference exceptions in detail. Honest selected-read states and material limits take precedence over copy or mockup fidelity. Full screen-placement and vocabulary ruling: `.agent/architect-W1-ux-answers.md`. This amends the W1-UX-01 disclosure-only proposal and preserves `D-2026-10-04-plain-language-analysis-views`; owner acceptance remains the combined W1 walkthrough.

## 2026-10-07

Decision: `D-2026-10-07-s1-foundation-import-and-cutover`. Codex Architect, responding to the S1-01 handoff review under the delegated decision authority in `.ai/ROLE_CONFIG.md`. The controlling detail is [the S1-01 Foundation contract addendum](../frontend/docs/architecture/s1-01-foundation-contract-addendum.md). This records an Architect choice, not owner acceptance of S1-01 or a new owner sign-off on the three Site-first documents. Current project routing treats those documents as implementation authority; their proposal headers remain provenance until an owner acceptance record is identified.

S1 uses PostgreSQL 16 through pinned Docker Compose, psycopg 3, SQLAlchemy Core and Alembic. A separate migrated PostgreSQL test database is required. The first migration enables `btree_gist` and applies the current-valid Foundation exclusion constraint; publication also locks the Site row. No application-only overlap fallback is silently selected. Initial Foundation may exist before bindings, but no acquisition snapshot or evidence is authorized until S1-02 publishes bindings.

Import the Kobo fixture under a configured development tenant, preserving `KOBO-001`, its timezone and v1 source values. Register all ten physical/topological components as assets; retain device inventory in Foundation content. Legacy signal mappings are comparison/candidate data until S1-02 turns them into explicit role bindings. Register one pending simulated gateway and one source per fixture device; one source owns one device report stream and all its signals share the source sequence scope. Gateway credentials remain unprovisioned until the S2 boundary. Internal database IDs are UUIDs; product routes keep the tenant-scoped stable Site code.

Until authentication is built, the backend resolves a fixed configured development tenant; client-supplied tenant identity has no authority. S1 cuts over Foundation only for explicitly imported Sites. The existing YAML Site file is the expected source of that import, identified by path and digest, not a duplicate collision. A changed source or another same-code record conflicts. Product Site index/detail reads select Postgres for registered imported codes (one row) and retain YAML for unimported codes. Legacy product Overview, Evidence and dispatch must not display or write stale file-backed state for an imported code. The Lab currently shares the Site repository and YAML store; it has no separate Site namespace. From import, new Lab Draft setup and existing Draft start/step/run-to-end/Commit for that code are blocked until Lab uses the registered snapshot. The current Stage endpoint is a read-only inspector; it may show historical staged envelopes labelled with their frozen YAML basis but cannot generate or seal more. Commit is blocked even for a stage produced before import. Evidence, Performance and Overview cut over in their later named slices. S1 publishing is battery reserve SOC only; priority controls stay read-only. The Kobo fixture has a 30-minute generator minimum-runtime property but no 35%/90% generator thresholds. An incomplete Foundation gap fixture removes reserve while remaining well-formed under the existing strict Site parser. The Planner owns the API/UI/guard details and records the fixture comparison in the S1-01 packet.

"""Dispatch recompute worker (S3-01 phase 2).

Leases the S2 `dispatch` recompute jobs, expands each job's source-time window
by the continuity bracket to the UTC-day assessment windows it can affect
(the affected window and its continuity neighbours), assesses each window at
the lease's knowledge cutoff through the typed seam, and commits every run,
revision and the job completion together. A failure releases the job for a
retry with back-off; after `max_attempts` the job is FAILED with its reason.

An acceptance that arrives while a job is leased cannot widen that job (S2
coalesces only into PENDING jobs), so it creates a new PENDING job: the next
pass reassesses with the new evidence and nothing is lost.

`reconcile` schedules windows whose last selected default run used another
model version or a configuration that the current selection no longer gives (a
backdated Foundation or binding publication); every `run_pending` pass runs it
for each assessed Site first.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import datetime, timedelta, timezone
from types import ModuleType
from typing import Any, Callable
from uuid import UUID

from assetops_backend.analytics import dispatch_v3

DOMAIN = "dispatch"
DAY = timedelta(days=1)
DISPATCH_ROLES = (dispatch_v3.GEN_STATE, dispatch_v3.GEN_POWER, dispatch_v3.LOAD, dispatch_v3.PV_AVAILABLE,
                  dispatch_v3.DISCHARGE, dispatch_v3.SOC)
ROLES_BY_SET = {s: tuple(roles_for(s)) for s in (ROLE_SET_MINIGRID_1, ROLE_SET_MINIGRID_2,
                                                    ROLE_SET_MINIGRID_3)}


def utc_now() -> datetime:
    return datetime.now(timezone.utc)


def day_windows(start: datetime, end: datetime, bracket: timedelta = BRACKET) -> list[Window]:
    """UTC-day windows intersecting `[start - bracket, end + bracket)`."""
    lo = (start - bracket).astimezone(timezone.utc)
    day = lo.replace(hour=0, minute=0, second=0, microsecond=0)
    out = []
    while day < end + bracket:
        out.append(Window(day, day + DAY))
        day += DAY
    return out


def configuration_sha256(config: Configuration) -> str:
    return sha256_json({
        "controls": sorted((c.payload() for c in config.controls), key=lambda p: sha256_json(p)),
        "bindings": sorted([b.role, str(b.asset_id), str(b.valid_from), str(b.valid_to),
                            str(b.binding_revision_id), b.role_set] for b in config.bindings),
        "role_sets": sorted(config.role_sets), "limits": sorted(config.limits),
    })


def input_basis(result: EvidenceQueryResult, model: ModuleType | Any = dispatch_v3) -> tuple[AnalyticInputSet, str]:
    """Cohort selection and the frozen input set of one query result, with its configuration digest.
    The worker assesses it; the phase-3 read compares its `input_sha256` with the selected run."""
    selection = select_cohort(result.readings, result.query.window, result.query.cohort, result.attestations)
    inputs = build_input_set(result, selection, analytic_id=model.ANALYTIC_ID, model_version=model.MODEL_VERSION,
                             roles_by_set=ROLES_BY_SET)
    config = Configuration(result.bindings, result.controls, result.role_sets, result.configuration_limits)
    return inputs, configuration_sha256(config)

Reads: accepted evidence (joined to its ACCEPTED disposition so the knowledge

binding sets, tenant- and Site-scoped and selected at the knowledge cutoff.

Writes, all in this module only: `analytic_run`, `analytic_run_evidence`,

    "SELECT e.id, e.envelope_sha256, e.batch_id, e.gateway_id, e.source_id, e.sequence_epoch, e.sequence, "
    "e.role, e.asset_id, e.source_time, e.interval_start, e.interval_end, e.published_at, e.received_at, "

def _attestation(r) -> ClockAttestation:
    return ClockAttestation(r[0], r[1], r[2], r[3], r[4], r[5], r[6], r[7], r[8], timedelta(milliseconds=r[9]),
                            r[10], r[11], r[12])


"""PostgreSQL Finding store (S4-01 phase 1, migration 0009).

The fifth product database writer (architecture guard rule 8): it only INSERTs
its own append-only tables. Writes for one Site run in one transaction under a
Site-scoped advisory lock and re-check the selection/job fence the matcher read
under, so a selection that lands between the read and the write is never
half-applied: the caller re-plans instead. Revisions are append-only and immutable
(`finding_revision_no_unique`); replay is idempotent against the Finding's latest revision only
(`_insert_revision`), so a basis may return (A -> B -> A, migration 0015) and append a new revision.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import json
from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import date, datetime
from typing import Any, Iterable
from uuid import UUID, uuid4

from sqlalchemy import create_engine, text
from sqlalchemy.engine import Connection, Engine
from sqlalchemy.exc import OperationalError, ProgrammingError

from assetops_backend.analytics.assessment_inputs import sha256_json
from assetops_backend.analytics.dispatch import CANDIDATE, INDETERMINATE, NECESSARY
from assetops_backend.analytics.finding_episode import (
    DOMAIN,
    MATCHER_VERSION,
    MECHANISM,
    TITLES,
    Episode,
    Write,
)


def _num(x) -> str:
    return format(float(x), ".4f")


class FindingStoreUnavailable(RuntimeError):
    pass


class FindingFenceMoved(RuntimeError):
    """A selection or recompute job changed between the matcher's read and its write."""


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Fence:
    selection_seq: int
    job_count: int
    job_marker: str
    finding_revisions: int = 0


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Applied:
    finding_ids: tuple[str, ...]
    revision_ids: tuple[str, ...]


class PostgresFindingStore:
    def __init__(self, database_url: str | None) -> None:
        self._database_url = database_url
        self._engine: Engine | None = None

    def _connect(self) -> Engine:
        if not self._database_url:
            raise FindingStoreUnavailable("DATABASE_URL is not set, so the Finding store cannot be reached.")
        if self._engine is None:
            self._engine = create_engine(self._database_url, pool_pre_ping=True)
        return self._engine

    def _read(self, work):
        try:
            with self._connect().connect().execution_options(isolation_level="REPEATABLE READ") as c:
                with c.begin():
                    return work(c)
        except (OperationalError, ProgrammingError) as error:
            raise FindingStoreUnavailable(f"The Finding store failed: {error.orig}") from error

    @staticmethod
    def _fence(c: Connection, tenant_id: UUID, site_id: UUID, analytic_id: str) -> Fence:
        seq = c.execute(text("SELECT coalesce(max(seq), 0) FROM analytic_run_selection WHERE tenant_id = :t "
                             "AND site_id = :s AND analytic_id = :a"),
                        {"t": tenant_id, "s": site_id, "a": analytic_id}).scalar_one()
        count, marker = c.execute(text(
            "SELECT count(*), coalesce(max(created_at)::text, '') || '/' || "
            "coalesce(string_agg(status, ',' ORDER BY id), '') FROM recompute_job WHERE tenant_id = :t "
            "AND site_id = :s"), {"t": tenant_id, "s": site_id}).one()
        # Finding state is part of the fence: a pass planned before another pass committed (whose episodes
        # are stale) must re-plan instead of opening a second Finding for the same episode. Revisions are
        # append-only, so their count only grows.
        revisions = c.execute(text("SELECT count(*) FROM finding_revision WHERE tenant_id = :t AND site_id = :s"),
                              {"t": tenant_id, "s": site_id}).scalar_one()
from assetops_simulator.packs.fuel import MINIMAL_FUEL_MODEL
from assetops_simulator.packs.mini_grid import (
    MINI_GRID_MODEL,
    MINI_GRID_V2_MODEL,
    MINI_GRID_V3_MODEL,
    MINI_GRID_V4_MODEL,
    MINI_GRID_V5_MODEL,
)
from assetops_simulator.packs.minigrid import DAYTIME_MINI_GRID_MODEL

#: Every model this build can execute, by the profile identity a run freezes.
#: Selected by identity, never by the shape of a run: the leaf is the one
#: place allowed to know both the product's profiles and the simulator's
#: models, so it is where a frozen profile identity becomes executable code.
EXECUTABLE_MODELS: tuple[ModelSpec, ...] = (
    MINIMAL_FUEL_MODEL,
    DAYTIME_MINI_GRID_MODEL,
    MINI_GRID_MODEL,
    MINI_GRID_V2_MODEL,
    MINI_GRID_V3_MODEL,
    MINI_GRID_V4_MODEL,
    MINI_GRID_V5_MODEL,
)


def model_for(run: SimulationRun) -> ModelSpec:
    """The executable model with the identity this run froze, or a refusal."""
    frozen = run.deterministic_identity.profiles
    for model in EXECUTABLE_MODELS:
        if (
            model.model_profile_id == frozen.model_profile_id
            and model.model_profile_version == frozen.model_profile_version
        ):
            return model
    raise FrozenRunNotReconstructible(
        f"Run {run.run_id} froze model profile {frozen.model_profile_id} "
        f"version {frozen.model_profile_version}, and this build has no model "
        "with that identity to execute it against."
    )


def publication_profile_for(run: SimulationRun) -> PublicationProfile:
    """The publication profile with the identity this run froze, or a refusal.

    T025b: the build carries two (versions three and four), so a run's readings
    are generated through the profile it froze rather than through whichever
    one the host was constructed with. The same rule as `model_for`.
    """
    frozen = run.deterministic_identity.profiles
    profile = find_publication_profile(
        PUBLICATION_PROFILES,
        frozen.publication_profile_id,
        frozen.publication_profile_version,
    )
    if profile is None:
        raise FrozenRunNotReconstructible(
            f"Run {run.run_id} froze publication profile "
            f"{frozen.publication_profile_id} version "
            f"{frozen.publication_profile_version}, and this build has no "
            "publication profile with that identity to generate its readings."
        )
    return profile

#: Which contract bound case a declared bound falls under, keyed on the state
#: whose value supplies the limit and the direction of the limit.
#:
#: Explicit rather than inferred. The two strings on this one row happen to
#: coincide, and that is a naming coincidence rather than the rule: nothing here
#: matches a bound to a case by spelling, for the same reason a bound is a
#: declaration rather than a shared prefix between two state keys. A declared
#: bound with no row is refused, so a second bound cannot arrive with a policy
#: nobody chose.
        self._registry = threading.Lock()

    def _model_for(self, run: SimulationRun) -> ModelSpec:
        """The model this run executes against.

        The injected one when a caller composed one - a test proving the
        identity check does - and otherwise the executable model whose identity
        the run froze (T024: there are two).
        """
        if self._model is not None:
            return self._model
        try:
            return model_for(run)
        except FrozenRunNotReconstructible as broken:
            raise LabControlRefused(
                "FROZEN_RUN_NOT_RECONSTRUCTIBLE", run.run_id
            ) from broken

    def _lock_for(self, run_id: str) -> threading.Lock:
        with self._registry:
            return self._locks.setdefault(run_id, threading.Lock())

    def _reporting_for(self, run: SimulationRun) -> FrozenReportingInputs:
        """The reporting inputs, or a typed refusal, and never a raw exception.

        **Every way this can fail is translated here, the cadence check
        included.** It used to be called separately in `start`, outside any
        translation, so a Draft whose timestep the fuel sensor's cadence does not
        divide - `timestep_minutes=30`, an input anybody would try - reached
        READY and then returned HTTP 500 on Start. The refusal's numeric
        explanation had already been written and never reached the user.

        The check belongs here rather than beside the call site because the
        object it is about is built here: a caller holding a
        `FrozenReportingInputs` should be holding one this run can express, and
        the only way to make that true is to refuse before returning it.
        """
        try:
            reporting = reporting_inputs(
                run, self._publication_profile, self._model_for(run)
            )
        except ExecutionContractIncompatible as incompatible:
            raise LabControlRefused(
                "CONTRACT_INCOMPATIBLE", run.run_id
            ) from incompatible
        invalid(
            f"'{where}.unit' must be {definition.unit!r} for property "
            f"{property_key!r} in {source}, got {unit!r}. The unit belongs to "
            "the property rather than to the document: the same quantity "
            "written in two units is two numbers a consumer would have to "
            "tell apart by reading text."
        )

    # The range this vocabulary gives the unit, where it gives one. A reserve
    # state of charge of 101 % parsed, persisted and rendered until an
    # independent review pointed at it.
    bounds = UNIT_RANGES.get(definition.unit)
    if bounds is not None and not bounds[0] <= value <= bounds[1]:
        invalid(
            f"'{where}.value' is {value} {definition.unit} in {source}, and "
            f"this vocabulary declares every {definition.unit} property "
            f"between {bounds[0]:g} and {bounds[1]:g}. That is a rule about "
            "the properties declared here rather than about the unit: the "
            f"only {definition.unit} property in it is a fraction of what a "
            "component holds, which cannot be more than all of it."
        )

    declared_source = _require_choice(
        mapping,
        "source",
        COMPONENT_PROPERTY_SOURCES,
        where=where,
        source=source,
        invalid=invalid,
    )

    source_version = mapping.get("source_version")
    if (
        type(source_version) is not int
        or not 1 <= source_version <= MAX_PROPERTY_SOURCE_VERSION
